However, the patient's significant loss of interest in previously enjoyed activities and withdrawal from social interactions, which have been present for a month, lean more towards Major Depressive Disorder. Yet, the direct connection to a significant life change and the relatively short duration of symptoms also keep Adjustment Disorder as a consideration. I just feel like I can't handle things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about when this major change occurred and how long you've been experiencing these feelings of low mood and anxiety?\n\nPatient: The major change happened about two months ago, and these feelings of anxiety and low mood started shortly after that. I've been struggling with them constantly for about a month now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest or pleasure in activities that you normally enjoy, since this major change occurred and you started feeling anxious and low?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've completely lost interest in the things I used to love doing, like spending time with my friends. Lately, I just end up staying at home and withdrawing from everyone because I don't have the energy or desire to do anything anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of low mood or depression for more than two years, or has this been a more recent development since the major change in your life?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't felt like this for two years at all. This is a very recent development that only started about a month ago, shortly after that major change in my life. Before that happened, I was actually doing quite well.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your low mood and anxiety are directly related to the major change you experienced, or are you experiencing distress that seems excessive or out of proportion to the change itself?\n\nPatient: Yes, my low mood and anxiety are definitely directly connected to that major change, and they started right after it happened. The distress feels incredibly intense and overwhelming, making it really hard for me to cope with my daily life.\n\nDoctor: Are you able to carry out your daily routines and responsibilities despite feeling overwhelmed, or have you had to significantly reduce your activities or rely heavily on others for support?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, but keeping up with my daily routines has definitely felt a lot more difficult and overwhelming lately. It has just been really hard to get things done, though I'm trying my best.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me a specific example of how your daily routines or responsibilities have been affected by your low mood and anxiety, such as work, school, or personal relationships?\n\nPatient: I can't really think of a specific concrete example right now. I've just generally been withdrawing from people and feeling too overwhelmed to handle things normally. Everything just feels like a blur lately.\n\nDoctor: Has your low mood and anxiety lasted for less than 6 months since the major change occurred, or are you concerned it might be lasting longer?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has definitely been less than six months. These feelings have only been going on for about a month now, which is well under that mark. The major change itself only happened about two months ago, so this is all still very recent."
  }

Both Major Depressive Disorder and Adjustment Disorder remain as candidates because the patient's symptoms could be consistent with either condition, given the recent onset and the clear precipitating event. However, the patient's report of significant impairment in daily life and loss of interest in activities might lean slightly more towards Major Depressive Disorder, but not enough to rule out Adjustment Disorder at this point.", "is_final": false}

The distress feels incredibly intense and overwhelming, making it really hard for me to cope with my daily life.\n\nDoctor: Are you able to carry out your daily routines and responsibilities despite feeling overwhelmed, or have you had to significantly reduce your activities or rely heavily on others for support?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, but keeping up with my daily routines has definitely felt a lot more difficult and overwhelming lately. It has just been really hard to get things done, though I'm trying my best.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me a specific example of how your daily routines or responsibilities have been affected by your low mood and anxiety, such as work, school, or personal relationships?\n\nPatient: I can't really think of a specific concrete example right now. I've just generally been withdrawing from people and feeling too overwhelmed to handle things normally. Everything just feels like a blur lately.\n\nDoctor: Has your low mood and anxiety lasted for less than 6 months since the major change occurred, or are you concerned it might be lasting longer?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has definitely been less than six months. These feelings have only been going on for about a month now, which is well under that mark. The major change itself only happened about two months ago, so this is all still very recent.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to start coping better and feeling more like yourself if the stress from the major change in your life were to be resolved or significantly reduced?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, absolutely, because my struggles are completely tied to this specific change. I really believe that if this stressful situation were resolved or got easier, my mood would improve and I'd be able to cope much better.

Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event. Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.
